### Page 7 (OCR, spell-checked: 4 words corrected)

```text
WARD-BECK SysTems LTD_
=
GENERAL
MicroCOM Communication System
The WBS Microcom Communication System is flexible, cost effective and ultra reliable intercom system for Radio and TV broadcasting fac- ilities_ Its control circuitry, includ ing that in the peripheral communication terminals, is microprocessor based . This allows for optimum flexibility in accommodating different operating philosophies with minimum of hardware variations bet ween systems. Likewise, the operational characteristics of an existing system may be altered by changing its software_
The central matrix of the MicroCoM system consists of one or more matrix blocks of 24 actively balanced audio inputs and 24 audio outputs System levels are normally set to +8dBm 600 ohms. AI connections to and from matrix block are via connectors, allowing the ease of expansion and/or the re-configuration of the central matrix of the Microcom system with little or no down time
Each matrix block contains Latch module which periodically refresh- es the storage capacitors of the audio crosspoints in an X-Y coordina- te fashion, thereby determining their state (on or off) These latches are driven off the microprocessor control system which receives its crosspoint selection data from the remote communication terminals _
The data transfer between the central matrix and the remote stations of the M7800 and M7810 series of primary communication terminals is via bi-directional balanced seri al data Line. Each of these terminals therefore only require three pairs of audio wires from the MicroCom matrix (input, output and control pairs) Secondary termi- nals of the M7820 and M7830 series may be slaved to primary stations and share their matrix buses in the central matrix.
Three types of test and monitor facilities for the Microcom system are optionally available:
a) matrix test terminal _ which permits the activation of any crosspoint of the central matrix for test purposes This terminal_ in conjunction with an output monitor bus, may also be used to Listen to any outgoing matrix bus. test select input on the isolation amplifiers at the matrix input is utilized to inject test tone or the audio output of the test terminal into the selected input bus
b ) status monitor provides visual indication of the activated crosspoints of selected output bus . This module is installed in the logic frame containing the output controllers and monitors the data stream going to the Latches in each of the matrix blocks_
1-1
```

### Page 9 (OCR, spell-checked: 2 words corrected)

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD_
e
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCoM Communication System
A ) Central Matrix Audio
.

The MFZD Matrix frames of the MicroCOM system form the modular building blocks, each containing up to 24 M724B Matrix Amplifiers _ AlI 24 balanced inputs of the M724B modules are bused together on printed circuit backplane assembly , which terminates on connectors at the rear of the MFZD frame. Each M724B Matrix Amplifier contains twenty-four solid state CMOS crosspoints, followed by an output amplifier_ This module al lows the selections of any or all of the 24 Line level inputs to be assigned and mixed to one common output.
direct input on the M724B matrix amplifier is provided for expand- ing communication buses beyond 24 inputs The outputs from success- ive matrix blocks are mixed to the inputs of primary matrix block
The crosspoints are activated by the control system via an X-Y co-ordinate type select philosophy This control is derived from the M771 Matrix Latch Module. See the sect ion "Central Matrix Control" for further details on the operation of the crosspoints.
The audio inputs of the central matrix may be wired to jackfield to provide patching facilities. From this point they connect to the M770 Quad Buffer Amplifier Modules which provide isolation between the matrix and the incoming sources.
These Buffer Amplifiers are housed in MFZDL mounting frame. AII terminations to this frame are on connectors similar to the MFZD ma trix blocks.
The M770 Buffer Amplifier provides either bridging or matched load to the input source Four isolated outputs are provided for optional inter-matrix distribution, Test select crosspoints are provided which permit the injection of test signal into any input bus This feature is utilized in the optional matrix monitor package outlined in following section.
The outputs of the MFZD matrix blocks may be wired to jackfield or terminated directly at terminal blocks at the rear of the equipment racks_ Additionally, the outputs may be paralleled to an optional output monitor bus.
2-1
```

### Page 11 (OCR, spell-checked: 3 words corrected)

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD.
 . ` : `I -`.. `.a
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCoM Communication System
B ) Central Matrix Control
The control system of the MicroCOM consists of processor based Input and Output Controllers Transmit/Receive and Matrix Latch Modules
series of micro- with associated
MicroCoM systems up to 24 inputs and 24 outputs require one Input and one Output Controller Module. In larger systems every 24 inputs require one M774A Input Controller and every 24 outputs require one M776A Output Controller Systems matrix sizes of 200 200 and beyond can be configured in this fashion
Each M774A Input Controller is associated with M772 Transmit/ Receive Module. The M772 sends commands to the communication termi- nals that are connected to its 24 balanced serial I/0 data ports and in turn receives their assignment and key data In systems with the calling tally feature this module also transmits the calling tally information back to the remote terminals  . The M774A Input Controller directs the flow of data between the remote terminals and the M772 Transmit/Receive Module. This data is transferred serially in three blocks of eight between the M772 and the Input Controller It is then re-formated and sent to all other control lers upon the request of Master Controller
This data transfer takes place on multiprocessor bus which connects to all controllers The Input Controllers transmit and receive data via this bus while the Output Controllers only receive the data . The data transfer on this bus is synchronized by an Output Controller that is assigned to be the Master . selector switch in the system allows the choice of any one of the Output Controllers to be Master This provides maximum of reliability since no one particular module is vital to overall system operation Since the system is divided into groups of 24 inputs and 24 outputs the failure of one module would only effect its own group without disturbing the remaining system.
The M776A Output Controllers assimilate and re-configure the data received from the Input Controllers. The data is assembled into crosspoint bit pattern and then transferred to the respective M771 Matrix Latch Modules . The M771's in turn control the crosspoint clos_ ures in the central matrix with this bit pattern.

2-3
```

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD_
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCom Communication System
M771 Matrix Latch Module is located in the first position of each MFZD matrix block . multi position frame selector switch on each M771 is set to decode its appropriate address, i.ej position for matrix inputs to 24 or position for inputs 25 to 48 etc
The audio crosspoints of an MFZD matrix block are activated by an X-Y type control from the M771 The crosspoint bit pattern of given output bus is loaded into these crosspoint control Latches _ The Bus Select Strobe of this bus is then activated, which in turn enables the control gates of the respective M724B Matrix Amplifier. This in turn refreshes the "memory capacitors" of the M724B which determine the crosspoint state. AMI 24 output buses are addressed in this fashion consecutively approximately every 150 milliseconds, with each bus select strobe being enabled for approximately 500 micro_ seconds
```

```text
WAAD-BECK Systems LTD_
s
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCoM Communication System
Since the crosspoint assignment data is received and continuously updated from the remote communication termina Is, data retention in the matrix control system is not necessary in the case of power failure. Upon recovery from power failure the system controllers will au tomatically reset, cycle through self-diagnostic program and then re-start their normal program. The assignment data is then up-dated from the remote comm unication terminals Note that the communication terminals include power fail circuit which retains the assignment data up to half an hour in case of power failure.
```

```text
WAAD-BECK Systems LTD_
s
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCoM Communication System
Since the crosspoint assignment data is received and continously updated from the remote communication termina Is, data retention in the matrix control system is not necessary in the case of power failure. Upon receovery from power failure the system controllers will au tomatically reset, cycle through self-diagnostic program and then re-start their normal program. The assignment data is then up-dated from the remote comm unication terminals Note that the communication terminals include power fail circuit which retains the assignment data up to half an hour in case of power failure.
```

```text
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCOM Communication System
C) Communication Terminals
 - 

  broad Line of Communication Terminals designed to interface with the central matrix, comp element the WBS MicroCOM Communication System . These may be divided into two categories, primary and secondary terminals. The primary terminals, known as the M7800 and M7810 series, interface directly wi= th the central matrix on three pairs of audio wires The secondary term inals, known as the M7820 and M7830 series, are slaved to the primary terminals and connect to the central matrix indirectly through the primary stations_
AII terminals are powered directly from the ac power ine and contain complex amplifiers for microphone, speaker and headset operation_ An auto levelling type preamplifier assures constant output level over wide range of input voice variations. Normal and an Overload LED indicator permit positive calibration of the front panel sensitivity control for each particular communication station transmit tally LED indicates when an audio signal is leaving the terminal Sidetone is provided for headset operation, giving the operator positive feedback of his own voice. The audio section of these terminals further includes solid state crosspoints for talkback operation between the primary and associated secondary terminals
The pric mary terminals contain microprocessor based control system which communicates with the central matrix directly on pair of audio wires. The bi-directional data transfer on this Line is based on RS422 format. It contains all assignment and key data for the selection of crosspoints in the central matrix. In systems with the cal ing tally feature the calling tally data is sent to the appro- private terminal on this Line. If the respective call ing station is assigned to fixed key , LED tally above the key will indicate the incoming call _ Mf the calling station is not programmed to fixed key and it is not presently selected on an assignable key . its cal ling code will appear flashing in the display window above the (or R) key  The call may then be answered by simply activating this lever key_
The key data transmitted to the central matrix may originate from either fixed key or an assignable key The fixed keys are pre-programmed to certain receiving station (or output bus in the central matrix) Their code selection is contained in an EPROM and may be changed by re-programm ing or replacing this memory chip.
```

```text
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCOM Communication System
```
```text
The assignable keys (A,B,C and D ) may be programmed to any valid recei ving station by entering its corresponding code number on the assignment keypad. seven-segment readout in the display window above the respective key will indicate the selected station code _ 2 It should be noted that audio of all incoming calls to communicate- ion terminal will always be heard regardless of assignment or tally functions
In addition to the normal operating voltages the M780 Power Supply which is utilized in the primary terminals, also contains nickel cadmium battery wie th trickle charger This battery provides back-up power for the RAM memory of the terminal' s control system for up to half an hour in case of power fai ure, thus retaining the selected assignment data.
The primary terminals are available in two basic configurations, the M7800 ser jes, which contains most necessary operational controls on the front panel , and the M7810 series, which includes all active circuitry but is designed to operate with remote key, assignment, microphone and speaker panels _
The M7800 series of communication terminals are designed for appli - cations in video tape, telecine areas or locations where this rack mounted unit can be positioned within easy reach of the operator.
In studio facilities the M7810 offers the flexibility of permitting operational controls to be strategically located in consoles or similar equipment, while the unit itself may be mounted in nearby rack or in console pedestal Various configurations of desk mounted panels to complement the M7810 series of communication terminals are availab le, such as the M781 Remote Assignment Module or the IKP series of intercom panels
The secondary terminals of the M7820 and the M7830 series are designed to operate in conjunction with one or more of the primary terminals and are utilized in appl ications such as telecine film islands or video tape production desks.
The audio circuitry of these units is similar of that of the primary terminals, but their inputs and outputs are connected to the associated primary termina Is. Solid state crosspoints in the primary terminals provide signal paths to and from the central matrix-
```

```text
WARD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD.
e cei .
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCoM Communication System
D) Option Facilities
Optional monitor and test facilities permit safe and efficient diag- nostic and serv icing procedures for the MicroCOM system without disturbing its normal operation.
The matrix monitor package includes M7710A matrix test terminal an output Listen bus and test select input. The M7710A is equipped similar to the primary communication terminals but its serial data output interfaces directly with the output controller modules of the central matrix, allowing it to select and activate any matrix crosspoint. Additionally an eight bit external control bus is extended from the M7710A . It drives Latch modules for the output Listen bus and the test select crosspoints of the M770 isolation amplifiers
To monitor any output bus, select "out" and the bus number of the desired output bus on the keypad of the test termina seven-segme- nt readout in the display window labelled "output" will indicate the selection. This activates the respective crosspoint in the output Listen bus which feeds the terminal's integral speaker and an optional vuU meter
The test select inputs of the isolation amplifiers are addressed from the terminal's keypad in similar manner . Selecting "N" and an input bus number activates the desired test select input. Again this selection is indicated in display window Label led iNPUT" test signal may be injected into the selected input bus by either activating the talk key for the terminal ' s integral microphone or the "OSC" key which injects test tone from an external oscillator .
Completing both the input and the output selection also activates third crosspoint, the matrix crosspoint of the chosen coordinate. The coordinate of crosspoint is established by both its input and its output bus number .
An input and output stepping feature has been provided to ease system alignment - Depressing the button label led TN ADV" on the keypad advances the input bus selection to the next input bus _ Likewise, depressing "OuT ADV" advances the output bus _ Each successive depression advances the selection by one, thereby consider- ably speeding up system alignment and trouble shooting
2-10
```

```text
SYSTEMS DESCRIPTION
MicroCOM Communication System
The optional M7720 Power Supply Monitor continuously scans all power supplies If power supply fails or if the supply voltage exceeds or drops below the allowable programmed Limits these voltage Limits are pre-programmed in an EPROM table and will vary for different systems application) , the following will occur:
a) The M7720 will stop its voltage scan and display both the voltage and the respective supply source on seven-segment readouts in the display window .
b ) The alarm status section of the display will spell the word "FAIL"
c) If the annunciator was selected to "ON" sound_
an audible alarm will
d) The fault tally of the respective supply source will be Iit.
e) The M7720 also provides an alarm tally output which may be utilized for visual and/or audible alarm in remote area
The alarm may be cancel led by depressing clear ("CLR") on the keypad.
The monitor will then continue its normal voltage scan but the fault indicator of the supply source in question will remain Iit. After restoring the supply voltage its source should be selected on the keypad and the monitor armed again for this supply by depressing "SET"
The power supply monitor also serves as digital voltmeter. Any supply voltage may be measured conveniently by simply selecting its source on the keypad_
```

```text
SERVICE INFORMATION
MicroCOM_Communication System
B ) Transmit Receive_Data
The M772 Data Transmit/Receive Module and its companion M774A Input Controller communicate simultaneously with up to 24 external communication terminals. Thus the data transfer between the M772 and M774A occurs in the form of bytes per transmitted or received serial data bit.   Refer to the M772 , M774A manuals and system interconnect drawings for the particular interconnection of data, address and control lines
This information may be observed by placing M772 on module extender and observing with an oscilloscope at connector pins through 14 for data, pins 69, 70 & 72 for the addresses, pin 74 for address select, pin 75 for NWDS and pin 76 for NRDS_
As in any common bused digital system the information on the data bus is synchronized with the appropriate address select and the read or write pulse. 
Note: The M774A/M776A controllers operate with clock frequency of 4MHz . This results in the read and write pulses having duration of S0Onsec , address selects with duration of approximately 60Onsec and data appearing on the data bus for 500 to 60Onsec . AlI levels are standard 5V TTL levels .   For stable waveform  externally trigger the osci  loscope on   . a) Address select, pin 74 of the M772 _ , 6 ) Key data command, pin 44 of the M774A Input Controller_
```

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD_
SERVICE INFORMATION
MicroCOM Communication System
c) Multi_Processor_Communications_Bus
This bus network passes data from an M774A Input Controller to all M776A Output Controllers as wel / as the rema ining input controllers if the calling tally feature is incorporated.   This bus network is divided into a) an Tine data bus   b ) an 11 Line address bus   c) control bus which consists of the following functions NWDS    Data Send Command_    Data Send addresses (3)    Acknowledge    Busy
The data is transferred just prior to the "receive key data" command _
Note: overall system control commands are issued from one of the output controllers that is selected to perform the master function This is determined by the Master Select switch which is located normally at the left hand side of the MFZDL logic mounting frame which contains the output controllers 
The duration of this data block varies in proportion to system demand but minimum of 26 data bytes are transferred per cycle. The data block transfer time is typically 1.5 milliseconds _
The data blocks may be observed by connecting the oscilloscope to the appropriate data Lines (M774A connector pins 63 to 70)
By triggering the oscilloscope on the "data send command" (pin 43 ) it wili be possible to observe all the data blocks being transferred between the input and output controllers_
If particular input controller is not present or active when the Data Transfer command is issued, "wait for acknowledge" is counted out by the output controller master (approximately milli- seconds) before the next data transfer command is issued.
```

```text
The duration of this data block varies in proportion to system demand but minimum of 26 data bytes are transferred per cycle. The data block transfer time is typically 1.5 milliseconds _
The data blocks may be observed by connecting the oscilloscope to the appropriate data Iines (M774A connector pins 63 to 70)
By triggering the oscilloscope on the "data send command" (pin 43 ) it wili be possible to observe all the data blocks being transferred between the input and output controllers_
If particular input controller is not present or active when the Data Transfer command is issued, "wait for acknowledge" is counted out by the output controller master (approximately milli- seconds) before the next data transfer command is issued.
3-4
```

</details>

### Page 27 (OCR, spell-checked: 3 words corrected)

```text
WARD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD.
 . - .
SERVICE INFORMATION
MicroCoM Communication System
E) Crosspoint_Data
The crosspoint bit pattern data at the output of the M771 Latch mod- ule is transferred to the associated M724B crosspoint module via 24 parallel data Tines _ It is strobe into each selected crosspoint module for period of L00 microseconds approximately every 150 milliseconds Therefore the status of each crosspoint is refreshed at this ra te, either being turned on or off _
The response time from activating Lever Key to the closure of crosspoint is in the order of 250 milliseconds. This is combination of system control or reaction time along with the deliberate soft switching action of the crosspoint. This timing allows for necessary overlap of either muting and/or unmuting functions to minimize the possibil ity of  regenerative feedback.
The Logic Levels into the M771 Latch module are standard 5V TTL (positive logic) , while the logic levels of the M771 Latch module outputs are at 12 volt level _ The strobe select outputs switch as negative logic (from 12v down to Ov ) and the crosspoint control outputs are switched as positive logic levels (O-OV crosspoint off, 1-12V crosspoint on )
Note: exercise extreme caution when operating this module on an ext- under card. Always turn off power before inserting or ex tracking this module via breaker SW2 on the module.
It is also necessary that the Frame Select switch SW1 is set to the correct code number for correct system operation.

3-6
```

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD_
SUMMARY
MicroCom Communication_System
AII microprocessor controllers of the MicroCoM system operate with the same general overall philosophy utilizing the National Semi - conductor microprocessor INS 8060 _
AII EPROM
are of the MM2516/2716 type (2Kx8 memory capacity)
RAMS are one of two types a) MM2114 (1Kx4) used on the SA14-013 communication terminal con trollers b ) MM2016 (2Kx8 ) used on M7744/M776A input and output controllers
The major logic components such as gates, buffers, Latches, decoders, etc. are from the 7400 series TTL Logic families_ Also some 8000 series components are used as wel| (e.g 8202 , 8208 ) major portion of the 8000 series may also be cross-referenced into the 7400 series of TTL Logic families.
Some CMOS Logic components are utilized to save power and realize the benefits of their operating characteristics. These are primarily of the 74C series manufactured by National Semiconductor.
Particular care should be taken that substitutions cannot be made between family, speed and power types without direct enquiry with the factory (e.9 standard power, low power and low power Schottky)
4_
```

```text
WAAD-BECK SysteMs LTD_
GENERAL
CROSSPOINT ADDRESSING
The Programming of the crosspoint control functions in the WBS Micro- com System for NTC Hollywood is divided into the following three groups:
a)
The programming for the fixed lever keys on the communicate- ions terminals and for the fixed pushbuttons on the assoc- iated panels.
b )
the program ing for the assignable lever keys on the communications terminals
the programming of the specialized crosspoint control funct- ions_
The fixed lever key or pushbutton operation of either talk or Listen crosspoint is determined by coding table located on the daughter assembly of the M774A input controller_ The M774A is located in the matrix control system.
The assignable lever key control functions are set by coding table located in each specific M7800 series communications terminal _
The specialized crosspoint control functions are programmed into EPROMS located on the daughter assembly of the M774A' and M776A controller modules_ The program located on the daughter assembly of the M774A input controller modules contains revised format routine that will shift all fixed lever key talk crosspoints on one input bus to different input bus_ The program located on the daughter assembly of the M776A output controller modules contains secondary control routine that is able to;
i )
utilize crosspoint operations to provide an external relay contact closure.
ii)
turn off certain talk crosspoints that appear when specific codes are assigned on an associated terminal
iii)
shift specific talk crosspoint operations on certain output buses from one input bus to another
iv)
shift specific talk crosspoint operations on certain input buses from one output bus to another_
```

### Page 44 (OCR, spell-checked: 2 words corrected)

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD.
s = ^.tc -.i
FIXED LEVER KEY
AND PUSHBUTTON
CROSSPOINT ADDRESSING
The addressing for fixed lever key and pushbutton control on communications terminal is determined by series of tables located within one 2716 EPROM memory chip. It is located on the 5A14-033 daughter assembly of the M774A input controllers
Each table corresponds to one block of 24 system control inputs The starting address of each table is located every 256 bytes (eg . OoooH , 0100H, 0200H, etc)
The first 24 locations of table represent index pointers for the quantity of fixed crosspoints controlled for each bus The rema ining 232 table locations are used for the actual bus codes that are utilized_
The index pointer not only gives the quantity of fixed crosspoints assigned but also whether the assignable function for the bus in question is activated. This is determined by setting bit to logic 1  If it remains logic then only the fixed address keys are activated_ An index poit nter code of 00 signifies no assignment on that bus in question.
Normally the total number of fixed address keys per bus is limited to 24, however with the addition of certain special control routines in the control system software, this total can be increased (e.9 NTC Supervisor)
The fixed lever key address code for talk control function is the bus number of the crosspoint to be activated It should be noted that all codes are stored in memory in hexadecimal format.
In the NTC Microcom System, one of the groups of stations, the Graphics station, has fixed pushbutton control as well as fixed lever key control
Each Graphics station has Graphics Select panel associated with its M7800 communications terminal This panel has three push- buttons which operate fixed Listen crosspoint on each of the three Graphics Party Line buses to allow the associated Graphics station to Listen to any one of the Graphics Party Lines .
2-1
```

```text
..s ` s

'
SPECIALIZED CROSSPOINT
CONTROL FUNCTIONS
-

The program that is loaded into the EPROM on the M774A input controller daughter assembly contains revised format routine (OUTBL ) that provides the operation of special crosspoint control functions.
These special control functions allow the NTC Supervisor station (bus 005 ) to operate total of 60 fixed lever keys on its associated bus In order to operate this quantity of fixed lever keys, two more input buses (bus 021 and bus 022 ) are dedicated to the supervisor station (bus 005 ) Key control from the 60 way Supervisor panel is connected to the communications terminal located on each of these [Wo buses   The purpose of the revised format routine is to shift any cross- point operation that appears on bus: 021 or bus 022 down onto the NTC Supervisor bus (005 ) thereby allowing the Supervisor station (bus 005 ) to talk to any station that is assigned to one of its 60 keys.            
   `        -  ;       .
```

### Page 48 (OCR, spell-checked: 1 words corrected)

```text
WAAD-BECK SYSTEMS LTD,
SPECIALIZED CROSSPOINT
CONTROL FUNCTIONS
The program that is loaded into the EPROM on the M776A output controller daughter assembly contains secondary control routine (SECT) which is able to:
i) scan all crosspoints located on the RF Intercom output bus (024) to check if any are switched on . If any crosspoints are on , status flag register will be set and used to drive relay K1 on the 82700-1 relay module. This relay module controls the direction of the signal path on the RF Intercom bus (024 )
ii) mask off any talk crosspoint operation that appears on the Page bus (002) as result of being assigned on any one of the four News terminals (bus 006 to bus 009 ) or on the NEWS FLASH CAMERA terminal (bus 011) . These stations should only be able to listen to bus 002  . iii ) shift certain Studio Select crosspoint operations so that the CSC Studio inputs (bus 079 to bus 087) can talk to the CSC station (bus 004) , the Supervisor Studio inputs (bus 070 to bus 078 ) can talk to the NTC Supervisor station (bus 005 ) and the Studio 63/64 Net Delay input (bus 068 and bus 069) can talk to the Net  Delay station (bus 012) _
With this ability to transfer crosspoint control operat ions, there is no need to have control terminal connected to any of the studio inputs (bus 068 to bus 087 ) since all crosspoint control comes from the terminal connected to the Studio Select inputs (bus 088 to bus 096 )
iv) shift certain Studio Select crosspoint operations from one output bus to another on the CSC (bus 004 ) and NTC Supervisor (bus 005 ) input lines
This allows the CSC (bus 004 ) and NTC Supervisor (bus 005 ) stations to talk to their associated studio Tines (bus 070 to bus 087 ) and also have calling tally information sent back to the appropriate key on the corresponding Studio Select terminal -
4-2
```
